## Semantics and scope

## Decisive mathematical checks

### 1. Regularity theorem: correct with its coherent finite hypotheses

Fix a finite, coherent, distinct 3-uniform hypergraph H on the twelve signed ports. For this Task's anchored circuit, every triple uses three distinct native axes, with one signed port on each selected axis. Every port has positive degree d(p); a triple incident to incoming p is chosen uniformly and its three legs receive equal shares. Let n(p,q) count triples containing both ports, including n(p,p)=d(p). In destination-row/source-column convention,

`K(q,p)=n(p,q)/(3*d(p))`.

Each column sums to one, the anchored diagonal is 1/3, the opposite port is excluded, and tagged turning mass is 2/3. Symmetry gives `K(q,p)*d(p)=n(p,q)/3=K(p,q)*d(q)` and `K*d=d`. Equal twelve-channel emission is preserved exactly when each incidence-connected component has constant degree. Different components may have different constant degrees.

I independently rederived the difficult direction without relying on the author's finite check. If each row sums to one, write x(p)=1/d(p). For each q,

`x(q) = [1/(3*d(q))] * sum_{h contains q} sum_{p in h} x(p)`.

At a component maximum this is an average of finitely many positive entries. Equality forces all entries in every incident triple to equal the maximum, and incidence connectivity propagates it throughout the component. Thus d is constant there. Conversely, constant component degree and absence of cross-component incidence give row sum one immediately. The argument uses the typed positive branch weights of the declared circuit, not an unproved native law.

The hypotheses matter. The row-balance/component-degree equivalence itself holds for any finite coherent 3-uniform H with positive degrees; the distinct-axis restriction additionally justifies opposite-port exclusion and the tagged turning interpretation used in this Task. Arbitrary incoming-dependent anchored families H_p need not form one coherent H; their row-mass formula survives but this degree criterion does not. Nonuniform edge/leg weights require their own statement. This theorem is not a universal native triad-admissibility test.

### 2. Source-labelled two-edge return: the corrected quantifiers are necessary

For a fixed positive conservative equal-three-leg kernel at each material, equal 1/12 incoming preparation, common attenuation `rho=12*lambda`, and no intervening gates or storage delay, write `r_a(q)=sum_p K_a(q,p)`. First emission in direction q is `lambda*r_a(q)`. Primitive travel enters the neighbor at **-q**, not q. The neighbor's return along -q uses its anchored diagonal 1/3, hence the occupied-neighbor factor is `4*lambda`, while an empty intermediate Cell contributes `lambda`.

Consequently the source-labelled two-edge own-return **total** is

`R_a^(2)=lambda^2 * [12 + 3*sum_{occupied-neighbor directions q} r_a(q)]`.

The old coefficient `(12+3*d_a)*lambda^2` holds for one specified layout exactly when that occupied-direction row sum equals its neighbor count. For every single-neighbor orientation under the same fixed kernel, it requires every row to be one. Pair/source-blind aggregation only forces the weaker `r(q)+r(-q)=2`; it does not establish either source's value separately.

This equation is about total response. The formula-only CWM count and dominant mass need not equal those of the full path CWM. The full trace, source labels, and full CWM triples remain separate evidence.

### 3. Fixed field and historical U2 audit

For a fixed positive column-conservative linear source/Cell/incoming transition operator T and `0<rho<1`, its l1 operator norm is at most one. The Neumann response solves the fixed linear field uniquely, with global total-response tail `N*rho^(L+1)/(1-rho)` after depth L for total injection N. Row balance is unnecessary. This does not promise finite all-depth branch counts or a finite CWM count; it does not apply unchanged to moving occupancy or unknown nonlinear storage/boundary rules.

I read the frozen proof and actual implementation for the prior U2 claims. The fixed-kernel, positive, linear, conservative, every-input packet-coverage quantifiers justify the 2/3 lower bound; the U1 adaptive particular-input construction is outside those quantifiers. The default 40 triples give the documented symmetric kernel, not a uniqueness theorem for all kernels. `transport_tables` and `field_prefix` use the default packet family and do not accept a custom-incidence field parameter. Custom syntactic triples are not checked for native action legality. Source labels remain in the internal field state, while returned `material_ports` merges sources and `self_return` is a total; neither output reconstructs the entire source-port matrix or original joint packet.

The P/Q triple-family separation preserves its declared marginal/pair/single-blocker equality and joint two-blocker distinction. Its controlled preparation discloses buffers of 1/40 and the remaining 39/40 reservoirs, with distinct external histories/boundaries. It proves neither spontaneous memory from identical closed preparations nor occupancy evolution. Source-blind directed-triple histograms are sufficient/necessary only for the declared whole-packet blocker and positive scalar-serial observation language. I do not transfer that claim to arbitrary later materials or source-sensitive observations.

Current canonical BRC-only policies were read before either scientific computation. It uses real BRC edge/serial/recoalescence/recurrent operations and reverses source/input/hyperedge/output traversal order. It checks exactly the existing five triples

Cells `0,E_1`, `lambda=1/48`, `rho=1/4`, depth 2. Original incidental parent indices were first verified against full parent prefixes; only enumeration order is normalized. There are 24, 90 and 1008 branch records at the three depths.

The row totals in signed port order are `4/3,1,5/6,1,5/6,1,7/6,1,7/6,1,2/3,1`. Returns are exactly `CWM(45,1/144,1/1728)` for source 0 and `CWM(48,5/768,1/3456)` for source 1. Reuse of immutable transition values reduces calls while keeping all branches separate. ## External prior art and deduplication

Six targeted external queries were actually run on 2026-10-15, followed by primary PDF reads. Exact queries, mappings and classifications are in the capsule's authored `PRIOR_ART_SCOPE_REVIEW.json`. The surfaces were external web search and original NIPS/PMLR papers, not merely the project catalog.

1. Zhou, Huang and Schölkopf, *Learning with Hypergraphs: Clustering, Classification, and Embedding* (2006), §4 equations (3),(4), PDF page 4: https://papers.nips.cc/paper_files/paper/2006/file/dff8e9c2ac33381546d96deea9922999-Paper.pdf . Uniform incident-edge then uniform within-edge selection, with unit edge weights and edge size three, is exactly the port kernel antecedent. Their source-row P is this report's K transposed. The component regularity criterion is a direct finite-component corollary of the standard degree stationary law, independently proved here; it is not new general hypergraph theory.
2. Chitra and Raphael, *Random Walks on Hypergraphs with Edge-Dependent Vertex Weights*, ICML/PMLR 97 (2019), Definition 3.1 equation (1) and the edge-independent special case equation (5), PDF pages 3 and 5: https://proceedings.mlr.press/v97/chitra19a/chitra19a.pdf . Unit weights reduce to the same kernel; the more general weighted setting is adjacent scope, not silently imported.

Exact kernel/stationary-law antecedents, the partial regularity corollary, adjacent weighted methods and generic hits with no material project-specific match are distinguished. Lack of another hit is not a novelty proof. Retained U2 value is the exact circuit assumptions, corrected source-labelled return/interface quantifiers, reproducible circuit witness, and honest source-gap boundary. No new general tool family or literature-wide novelty is claimed. Raw third-party papers/search text remain local and are not republished in this capsule.

## Portable evidence capsule

The appendix contains a deterministic gzip/base64 JSON capsule with per-member byte/SHA-256/Git-blob pins and full reversible project evidence. Its sources remain individually pinned in custody ledgers; paths in old execution receipts are historical location claims, not required extraction targets. Capsule extraction alone runs no scientific code. To reproduce the independent check, restore `inputs` as its `--inputs-root` and choose a new empty `--output-root`; the original checker replay is a separate already-completed verification. Historical custody flags reflect their actual earlier timestamps; the later ACTUAL check records establish the completed runs.

`{"gzip": {"bytes": 121966, "git_blob_sha1": "114380866d9848448b8f832a27803a8400b6d67b", "sha256": "0e2596a6946cb3fdc715ebda310301f2b189e8d6b0916850565714dd2553a5d3"}, "member_bytes": 259467, "members": 33, "uncompressed": {"bytes": 279846, "git_blob_sha1": "43af08d299166cbe0bfdec06f35ae3b1cb7f3830", "sha256": "f7dd0efbc1f212b5109caa338efb2d349255f3b503c2d7766c915af33d0360c7"}}`

To decode safely, copy the appendix's base64 characters to `capsule.b64`, then in a new working directory run the following standard-library snippet. It validates every member before writing and rejects absolute/traversing paths. This is an administrative decoder, not a propagation method.

```python
import base64, gzip, hashlib, json
from pathlib import Path
raw = gzip.decompress(base64.b64decode(Path('capsule.b64').read_text()))
assert hashlib.sha256(raw).hexdigest() == 'f7dd0efbc1f212b5109caa338efb2d349255f3b503c2d7766c915af33d0360c7'
data = json.loads(raw)
dest = Path('u2_review_evidence')
assert not dest.exists()
decoded = []
for row in data['files']:
    rel = Path(row['path'])
    assert not rel.is_absolute() and '..' not in rel.parts
    assert row['encoding'] in {'utf-8', 'base64'}
    body = row['data'].encode('utf-8') if row['encoding'] == 'utf-8' else base64.b64decode(row['data'], validate=True)
    assert len(body) == row['bytes']
    assert hashlib.sha256(body).hexdigest() == row['sha256']
    assert hashlib.sha1(b'blob ' + str(len(body)).encode() + b'\0' + body).hexdigest() == row['git_blob_sha1']
    decoded.append((rel, body))
for rel, body in decoded:
    path = dest / rel
    path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    path.write_bytes(body)
```
